# SUMMARIES OF BOOK II

*Concerning Zyto the Bohemian.* <note>Zyto was a legendary 14th-century sorcerer at the court of King Wenceslaus IV.</note>
*Concerning magical herbs.*
*The deceptions of a certain modern traveling performer.*
<margin>C</margin> *Examples of true effects.*
*The charming of serpents.*
*Signs of the False Prophets in the Gospel.*
*Was Samuel truly raised by the Witch of Endor? The passage in 1 Samuel 28 and Ecclesiasticus 46:20.* <note>original: "1. Reg. 28. Eccl. 46. v ult." This refers to the theological debate over whether the ghost of the prophet Samuel was real or a demonic mask.</note>
*Concerning the effects of Pharaoh’s Mages, Exodus 7:11.*
*How the staff was changed.*
<margin>D</margin> *Concerning mixed effects, with sacred examples related thereto.*
*Concerning the contest between two mages, from Trithemius.* <note>Johannes Trithemius was a famous Renaissance abbot and occultist.</note>
<margin>E</margin> *How these effects are to be discerned: two rules.*

### Question 7. Can mages perform any true miracle?

<margin>A</margin> *The erroneous affirmative opinion of many.*
<margin>B</margin> *How a miracle and a wonder differ and ought to be distinguished.* <note>The author distinguishes between a "miracle" (supernatural act of God) and a "wonder" (natural but hidden or demonic trickery).</note>
<margin>C</margin> *Miracles cannot be performed except by God alone.*
*Faith is effectively proven by miracles.*
*Queen Elizabeth of England’s curing of the "King’s Evil."* <note>original: "curatio strumaria." Refers to scrofula, which monarchs were traditionally believed to heal by touch.</note>
<margin>D</margin> *Mages cannot perform absolute wonders; the passage in Psalm 71:18.*
<margin>E</margin> *They can perform wonders that are such only in respect to us.*
*Concerning the birth of women in the Kingdom of Naples, whose decisive offspring are preceded by little frogs.*
*Why could Nero perform nothing wonderful through Magic?*
*Concerning the miracles of the Antichrist. Also of the Calvinist heretics, etc.*

### Question 8. In what way do mages accomplish these wonders through the devil?

<margin>A</margin> *What he can do through local motion.*
*He cannot move an entire element from its place.*
*The passage of Firmilian explained.*
*He fakes transformations through speed, such as that of Iphigenia and other pagans.*
<margin>B</margin> *Aclorius uncovered this fraud in Phoenicia.*
*What he can do by applying active forces to passive subjects.*
<margin>C</margin> *In what way the demon is the cause here, and how the force of nature must be considered and carefully investigated.*

---

*Wonders performed through prestige.* <note>term: "prestige" (praestigia) here refers to illusions or sleight-of-hand, not social status.</note>
*Concerning the triple mode of deception: by changing the object, the medium, or the organ.*
*Concerning the manifold change of the object: various examples.*
*Through a hole in the side, images are seen which are bare and unformed lines.*
<margin>D</margin> *Straw and rushes, though unmoved, appear to be dancing serpents.*
*Concerning the change of the medium.*
*So that ghosts appear to be present.*
*So that people appear to have the heads of donkeys.*
*A coin at the bottom of a bowl appears to be on the surface.*
*A rooster with a straw appears to be dragging a heavy beam.*
*How the Vestal Virgin Claudia pulled a ship with her belt.*
*Various appearances through looking-glasses.*
*A thing appears present when it is absent and remote.*
*The nature of the philosopher’s vision in St. Augustine.*
<margin>E</margin> *Concerning deception by reason of the organ.* <note>Refers to the sensory organs, specifically the eyes.</note>
*So that absent things appear present.*
*So that what is single appears double.*
*Concerning the "blindness" of the Sodomites, Genesis 19.* <note>original: "äoraſia" (aorasia), a Greek term for spiritual blindness or the inability to see what is right in front of one.</note>
*Concerning the illusions of Zedekiah the Jew.*
<margin>F</margin> *A rule for discerning illusions from true effects.*
*How they make a river suddenly appear in a bedroom.*
*They appear to devour horses and restore them.*
*They appear to send in great serpents, as Michael Sicidites did.*
*The illusions of the heretic Eon.*
*The festive trickery of the Pole Jacob Mestinski through simulated miracles.*
*The illusions of a traveling performer in Apuleius.*
